Or that I mishandled some family photos, or... let someone in the community down. I know I've already been over it, but... I just can't seem to let it pass.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to do certain things over and over, like checking or counting?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I don't think I avoid things. I still do my work and go where I need to go. I just... I just have to do the checking first, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any physical actions repeatedly, like washing your hands or locking doors?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. I don't... I don't do any of that. Just the things we already talked about.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, aside from worrying about mistakes or replaying conversations?\n\nPatient: I... well, sometimes. Just... personal things. Like, even if I'm right in the middle of trying to work through a system design, or... prepare a lesson, these thoughts just barge in. I'll start worrying that I... sent the wrong message to my ex-wife, or maybe mishandled some family photos. Or that I let someone in the community down. It's... I don't know. I know I've already been over it all in my head, but I just can't seem to let it pass.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these habits of checking and replaying conversations are interfering with your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes, it does interfere. My work has... well, it's slowed down. I've missed some design milestones lately. And I've had to push back some mentoring sessions I usually handle without any issue. \n\nIt's just... it takes up time. I even showed up late to a youth coding workshop I was supposed to help run. I wasn't prepared for it at all. It was... embarrassing, to be honest. It's something that's important to me, and I just... couldn't get it together.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, aside from worrying about mistakes or replaying conversations, that you feel are intrusive or unwanted?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just... bothersome, I guess. \n\nIt's frustrating when I'm just trying to get my work done and... well, yeah. It's just something I have to deal with. I haven't really thought about it much beyond that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a significant sense of fear or anxiety when these intrusive thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: Not... fear, exactly. I wouldn't say that. It's more of a... a tension, maybe? Just feeling unsettled until I go back and check. But I wouldn't call it a significant anxiety. It's just... annoying.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking or replaying these things helps to reduce your anxiety or tension?\n\nPatient: I suppose it does. A little. But... well, that's actually why I came in to see you. \n\nI just keep losing chunks of time to it. I'll sit there re-reading emails and texts, or... reopening folders on my computer. Or I'll be mentally replaying conversations before I can leave the house or start my work. \n\nI guess... I'm just trying to prove to myself I didn't make some serious mistake. So, doing all that settles the tension, but... it's just taking up too much of my day.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that are not about mistakes, but still feel intrusive or unwanted?\n\nPatient: I... well, sometimes.
